## GHSA-98xx-8mx4-x7cm · vm2

2026-10-01 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The vm2 library's NodeVM allowed sandboxed code to call `tls.setDefaultCACertificates()`. This function, when called from within the sandbox, would modify the host process's global TLS trust store, effectively allowing the sandboxed code to influence the security of the entire host application's TLS connections.

### Impact

An attacker could replace the host process's default CA trust store, enabling them to intercept and decrypt TLS traffic from the host application by presenting attacker-signed certificates. This leads to a complete compromise of the host's TLS security.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/builtin.js
+++ b/lib/builtin.js
@@ -210,6 +210,163 @@ if (EventEmitter.EventEmitterAsyncResource) {
 
 // SECURITY (GHSA-98xx-8mx4-x7cm): `tls.setDefaultCACertificates(list)` replaces
 // the calling thread's process-wide default CA trust store, so every subsequent
-// host TLS client that doesn't supply its own `ca` accepts attacker-signed
-// certificates. This is the same process-wide-mutation class as the already-
-// denied `dns.setServers`; unlike dns the rest of `tls` is legitimately useful
-// to sandboxed code, so neutralize just this member. (The native function
-// requires a real host array, which the sandbox can forge via `url`'s
-// `URLSearchParams.getAll()` — the bridge unwraps it back to a host array — so
-// argument-side defenses are insufficient; the member itself must be removed.)
+function sanitizeTlsModule(mod) {
+	if (typeof mod.setDefaultCACertificates !== 'function') return mod;
+	const copy = Object.assign({}, mod);
+	copy.setDefaultCACertificates = function setDefaultCACertificates() {
+		throw new Error('tls.setDefaultCACertificates is disabled in vm2 sandboxes: it replaces the host process default CA trust store (GHSA-98xx-8mx4-x7cm).');
+	};
+	return copy;
+}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `sanitizeTlsModule` function that intercepts calls to `tls.setDefaultCACertificates`. Instead of forwarding the call to the host function, it now throws an error, preventing sandboxed code from modifying the host's TLS trust store.

## GHSA-h85j-hv3c-qfgq · vm2

2026-10-01 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 10.0 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→STACK_TRACE`

### Root cause

The vm2 sandbox exposed host process-wide singletons like `http.globalAgent` and `https.globalAgent` directly to sandboxed code. While these were wrapped as 'read-only', the `readonly()` proxy only prevents property *assignment*, not method calls or event subscriptions. This allowed the sandbox to subscribe to events on the host's global agents.

### Impact

An attacker could subscribe to events on the host's `globalAgent` objects, allowing them to observe sensitive information such as HTTP/HTTPS request options (including authorization tokens, private host/port details) and released TLS sockets from unrelated host requests, leading to credential and traffic exfiltration.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/builtin.js
+++ b/lib/builtin.js
@@ -210,6 +210,163 @@ if (EventEmitter.EventEmitterAsyncResource) {
 	EventEmitterReferencingAsyncResourceClass = EventEmitterReferencingAsyncResource;
 }
 
+// SECURITY (GHSA-h85j-hv3c-qfgq): `http.globalAgent` / `https.globalAgent` are
+// the real process-wide host singletons. The read-only wrap hands them straight
+// to the sandbox, and `.on('free'|'keylog'|...)` is a *read*+subscribe, not a
+// property assignment, so it is forwarded to the host object. A sandbox listener
+// then receives live host request options (Authorization tokens, private
+// host/port) and the released host TLSSocket whenever an unrelated host request
+// completes — credential/traffic exfiltration. Replace the exposed `globalAgent`
+// with a fresh sandbox-dedicated Agent so the sandbox can never reach the host
+// singleton.
+function makeHttpAgentSanitizer(agentKey) {
+	return function sanitizeHttpModule(mod) {
+		if (typeof mod.Agent !== 'function' || !mod[agentKey]) return mod;
+		const copy = Object.assign({}, mod);
+		const sandboxAgent = new mod.Agent();
+		// The exposed globalAgent is the sandbox-dedicated one — a direct read +
+		// `.on('free')` reaches only this empty agent, never the host singleton.
+		copy[agentKey] = sandboxAgent;
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces the exposed `http.globalAgent` and `https.globalAgent` with fresh, sandbox-dedicated `Agent` instances. It also modifies the `request()` and `get()` methods of the `http` and `https` modules to default to using these sandbox-dedicated agents, preventing sandboxed code from interacting with the host's shared agents.

## GHSA-46pr-c5wc-xffx · vm2

2026-10-01 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.9 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→RCE`

### Root cause

The vm2 sandbox allowed access to certain Node.js built-in modules (like `crypto`) without properly sanitizing or restricting dangerous functions. Specifically, `crypto.setEngine()` could be called by sandboxed code, which then instructed OpenSSL to dynamically load a native library from a specified path into the host process. The constructor of this native library would execute arbitrary code before OpenSSL even validated it as a legitimate engine.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary native code execution on the host system, effectively escaping the vm2 sandbox and gaining full control over the host process.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/builtin.js
+++ b/lib/builtin.js
@@ -210,6 +210,163 @@ if (EventEmitter.EventEmitterAsyncResource) {
 	EventEmitterReferencingAsyncResourceClass = EventEmitterReferencingAsyncResource;
 }
 
+// SECURITY (GHSA-46pr-c5wc-xffx): Some builtins are safe to expose EXCEPT for a
+// handful of members that reach host-process authority the `vm.readonly()` wrap
+// cannot contain. readonly() blocks property *assignment* through the sandbox
+// proxy, but it forwards every *call* to the host member with full host
+// authority -- so a callable that loads native code, mutates a process-wide
+// security setting, or hands back a shared host singleton is a sandbox-escape
+// primitive even behind the read-only proxy. Rather than deny these
+// otherwise-useful modules wholesale (hashing/signing is a legitimate sandbox
+// use of `crypto`), expose a sanitized shallow copy with just the dangerous
+// member neutralized.
+//
+//   - crypto.setEngine(path[, flags]) : hands `path` to OpenSSL's ENGINE loader,
+//     which asks the OS dynamic loader to load the named shared library. The
+//     library's constructor runs as arbitrary native code BEFORE OpenSSL decides
+//     whether the file is a usable engine -- so even the expected
+//     ERR_CRYPTO_ENGINE_UNKNOWN rejection happens only after host-native code has
+//     already executed. A sandbox with only `crypto` allowed and a native file in
+//     its own package directory therefore has a native-RCE primitive.
+//
+// The stub throws instead of forwarding to host OpenSSL, so no library is ever
+// loaded. Matching strips the `node:` prefix so `node:crypto` shares fate.
+function sanitizeCryptoModule(mod) {
+	const copy = Object.assign({}, mod);
+	copy.setEngine = function setEngine() {
+		throw new Error('crypto.setEngine is disabled in vm2 sandboxes: it asks OpenSSL to dynamically load a native library into the host process, executing arbitrary native code (GHSA-46pr-c5wc-xffx).');
+	};
+	return copy;
+}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `sanitizeCryptoModule` function that intercepts calls to `crypto.setEngine()`. Instead of forwarding the call to the host's OpenSSL, it now throws an error, preventing the dynamic loading of native libraries and thus neutralizing the sandbox escape vector.

## GHSA-6w8r-xxw2-g3hx · vm2

2026-10-01 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.9 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→RCE`

### Root cause

The vm2 sandbox failed to properly restrict access to certain Node.js built-in modules, specifically `node:sqlite`. The `DatabaseSync` constructor in `node:sqlite` allowed the `allowExtension` option to be set, which, if enabled, permits loading native SQLite extensions. This capability was not adequately neutralized by the sandbox's read-only proxy, enabling a sandboxed plugin to execute arbitrary native code in the host process.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary native code on the host system, effectively escaping the sandbox and achieving full remote code execution (RCE) with the privileges of the Node.js process.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/builtin.js
+++ b/lib/builtin.js
@@ -210,6 +210,163 @@ if (EventEmitter.EventEmitterAsyncResource) {
 
+// SECURITY (GHSA-6w8r-xxw2-g3hx): `node:sqlite`'s DatabaseSync can load a native
+// SQLite extension (`loadExtension(path)` / the `loadExtension` SQL function) —
+// arbitrary native code in the host process. Node gates this entirely on the
+// constructor's `allowExtension` option: with it off (the default), both
+// `loadExtension()` and `enableLoadExtension()` throw `ERR_INVALID_STATE`.
+// Wrap the DatabaseSync constructor so `allowExtension` is forced off, which
+// closes every extension-loading path while leaving normal SQL usable.
+function sanitizeSqliteModule(mod) {
+	const HostDatabaseSync = mod.DatabaseSync;
+	if (typeof HostDatabaseSync !== 'function') return mod;
+	const copy = Object.assign({}, mod);
+	class DatabaseSync extends HostDatabaseSync {
+		constructor(location, ...rest) {
+			// Preserve call arity (native DatabaseSync rejects an explicit
+			// `undefined` options arg). Force `allowExtension` off only when an
+			// options value is actually supplied; otherwise the native default
+			// (off) already applies. SECURITY (GHSA-6w8r-xxw2-g3hx follow-up): the
+			// native DatabaseSync also accepts a FUNCTION as its options argument
+			// (functions carry own properties), so `function o(){}; o.allowExtension
+			// = true` bypassed an `object`-only check. Treat functions as options
+			// too — Object.assign copies their own enumerable props and forces
+			// allowExtension off.
+			if (rest.length > 0 && rest[0] !== null &&
+				(typeof rest[0] === 'object' || typeof rest[0] === 'function')) {
+				rest[0] = Object.assign({}, rest[0], {allowExtension: false});
+			}
+			super(location, ...rest);
+		}
+	}
+	copy.DatabaseSync = DatabaseSync;
+	return copy;
+}
+
+// SECURITY (GHSA-98xx-8mx4-x7cm): `tls.setDefaultCACertificates(list)` replaces
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `sanitizeSqliteModule` function that wraps the `node:sqlite.DatabaseSync` constructor. This wrapper forces the `allowExtension` option to `false` when an options object is provided, preventing the loading of native SQLite extensions. This ensures that even if a sandboxed plugin attempts to enable extensions, the underlying host functionality will deny it.

## GHSA-c48m-32m9-vx93 · vm2

2026-10-01 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.9 · Pattern: `UNCLASSIFIED`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an insufficiently strict regular expression used to validate allowed external package names. The regex allowed partial matches, meaning a malicious package name like 'evil-left-pad' could bypass the allowlist if 'left-pad' was permitted. Additionally, even with an anchored regex, path traversal sequences ('..') within subpaths of allowed packages were not explicitly forbidden, allowing an attacker to escape the intended package and load an arbitrary host package.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass the `vm2` sandbox and execute arbitrary code in the host environment with the privileges of the Node.js process running the sandbox.

### Key diff

```
- this.externalCache = externals.map(pattern => new RegExp(makeExternalMatcherRegex(pattern)));
+ this.externalCache = externals.map(pattern => new RegExp('^(?:' + makeExternalMatcherRegex(pattern) + ')(?:[\\/].*)?$'));
+ if (x.split(/[\/]/).indexOf('..') !== -1) return undefined;
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces two main fixes: first, it anchors the regular expression used for external package allowlisting to ensure it matches the entire package name, optionally followed by a subpath. Second, it explicitly checks for and rejects any package specifier containing '..' path segments before calling the custom resolver, preventing path traversal attacks.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 769 occurrences**

## GHSA-qhwx-74w5-xhxq · vm2

2026-10-01 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.9 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vm2 sandbox environment failed to properly restrict access to the 'node:test' built-in module. This module, when invoked with specific `execArgv` parameters, could spawn a separate Node.js process outside the sandbox, executing attacker-controlled code with full host privileges.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution on the host system, completely escaping the vm2 sandbox and gaining full control over the environment.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/builtin.js
+++ b/lib/builtin.js
@@ -162,7 +162,20 @@ const DANGEROUS_BUILTINS = new Set([
 	// `os.platform()`, `os.EOL`, `os.constants`) can register a controlled
 	// wrapper under the same name via `mock` / `override`.
 	'os',
-	'dns'
+	'dns',
+	'test'
 ]);
 
 // SECURITY (GHSA-rp36-8xq3-r6c4): Family-prefix denylist check. `inspector` and
@@ -171,7 +184,10 @@ const DANGEROUS_BUILTINS = new Set([
 // `node:process` and `node:inspector/promises` cannot bypass via spelling.
 function isDangerousBuiltin(key) {
 	if (typeof key !== 'string') return false;
-	if (key.startsWith('node:')) key = key.slice(5);
+	while (key.startsWith('node:')) key = key.slice(5);
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds 'test' to the `DANGEROUS_BUILTINS` denylist, preventing its use within the sandbox. It also enhances the `isDangerousBuiltin` function to strip all leading 'node:' prefixes from module names, preventing bypasses via double-prefixed spellings like 'node:node:test'.

## GHSA-27g9-p43v-cw3v · vm2

2026-10-01 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.8 · Pattern: `TYPE_CONFUSION→BYPASS`

### Root cause

The vm2 sandbox's `Promise.prototype.then` and `catch` overrides were installed using direct assignment. On Node.js 26 (V8 14.6), this method of installation did not invalidate the `PromiseThenLookupChain` protector. Consequently, native `Promise.prototype.finally` trusted the stale protector and invoked the original native `then` method, bypassing the sandbox's wrapper and its `resetPromiseSpecies` call. This allowed an attacker to install a malicious `constructor[Symbol.species]` on a promise and gain control over the native reaction's resolve/reject logic, leading to a sandbox escape.

### Impact

An attacker could escape the vm2 sandbox, gaining arbitrary code execution in the host Node.js environment.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/setup-sandbox.js
+++ b/lib/setup-sandbox.js
-globalPromise.prototype.then = function then(onFulfilled, onRejected) {
-globalPromise.prototype.catch = function _catch(onRejected) {
+const wrappedPromiseThen = function then(onFulfilled, onRejected) {
+const wrappedPromiseCatch = function _catch(onRejected) {
+localReflectDefineProperty(globalPromise.prototype, 'then', {
 	__proto__: null,
 	value: wrappedPromiseThen,
 	writable: true,
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies how `Promise.prototype.then`, `catch`, and `finally` wrappers are installed. Instead of direct assignment, `Reflect.defineProperty` is now used to ensure the `PromiseThenLookupChain` protector is invalidated. Additionally, `Promise.prototype.finally` is now also wrapped to call `resetPromiseSpecies` before delegating to the native implementation, independently closing this specific species channel.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 11 occurrences**
> Languages: JavaScript, RUST, Erlang, Go
> → GHSA-g8f2-4f4f-5jqw
> → GHSA-429q-fhh4-r6hj

## GHSA-9cqf-hhrq-7v45 · github.com/siyuan-note/siyuan/kernel

2026-10-01 · Go · **HIGH** 8.6 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The `getAttributeViewSearchTarget` API endpoint was accessible without proper authorization checks. This allowed unauthenticated or unauthorized users to retrieve database row content by providing specific `id` and `keywords` parameters, effectively bypassing the intended access controls.

### Impact

An attacker could access sensitive database row content that they are not authorized to view, leading to information disclosure. This could expose private user data or system configurations.

### Key diff

```
-export const openSearchEditor = (options: {
+export const openSearchEditor = async (options: {
     protyle: IProtyle,
     openPosition?: string,
     id: string,
     rootId: string,
-    cb: () => void
+    cb: () => void,
+    nodeType?: string,
+    method?: number,
+    keywords?: string[],
+}) => {
+    if (options.nodeType === "NodeAttributeView" && typeof options.method === "number" && [0, 1, 3].includes(options.method) &&
+        options.keywords?.length > 0) {
+        try {
+            const response = await fetchSyncPost("/api/av/getAttributeViewSearchTarget", {
+                id: options.id,
+                keywords: options.keywords,
+            });
+            if (response.code === 0 && response.data) {
+                const opened = await openDatabaseRowByData(options.protyle, response.data as IDatabaseRowOpenData, {
+                    position: options.openPosition,
+                });
+                if (opened) {
+                    options.cb?.();
+                    return;
+                }
+            }
+        } catch (e) {
+            console.warn("Resolve attribute view search target failed:", e);
+        }
+    }
+    openSearchBlockEditor(options);
+};
+
+const openSearchBlockEditor = (options: {
     protyle: IProtyle,
     openPosition?: string,
     id: string,
     rootId: string,
     cb: () => void,
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `openSearchEditor` function that checks the `nodeType`, `method`, and `keywords` before calling `getAttributeViewSearchTarget`. It also adds `data-node-type` attributes to search results, ensuring that the `getAttributeViewSearchTarget` endpoint is only called under specific, authorized conditions, and moves the original logic to `openSearchBlockEditor`.

## GHSA-jxxv-8r27-vm4p · vm2

2026-10-01 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.6 · Pattern: `INSECURE_DEFAULT→CONFIG`

### Root cause

The vm2 CLI, when executing a script, configured the sandbox to allow external requires without restricting the root path or context. This meant that sandboxed code could use `require()` to load arbitrary modules from the host system, effectively breaking out of the sandbox.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary code on the host system by crafting a malicious script that leverages the unrestricted `require()` function to load and execute host-level modules.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/cli.js
+++ b/lib/cli.js
 			verbose: true,
 			require: {
-				external: true
+				external: true,
+				root: pa.dirname(path),
+				context: 'sandbox'
 			}
 		});
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the vm2 CLI to explicitly set `require.root` to the script's directory and `context` to 'sandbox'. This ensures that `require()` calls from within the sandboxed script are confined to the script's own directory and executed within the sandbox, preventing access to host-level modules. Additionally, defense-in-depth measures were added to prevent sandboxed code from requiring vm2 itself.

## GHSA-6966-vjj6-99xv · jupyterlite-core

2026-10-01 · Python · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

JupyterLab allowed content pasted from the system clipboard to be treated as 'trusted' cells, even if the content originated from an untrusted source. This meant that malicious HTML or JavaScript embedded in cell outputs could be executed when pasted, as the application did not differentiate between internal clipboard operations and external system clipboard pastes.

### Impact

An attacker could craft malicious notebook cell content, copy it to their system clipboard, and then trick a victim into pasting it into their JupyterLab instance. This would lead to the execution of arbitrary JavaScript in the victim's browser, potentially allowing for session hijacking, data exfiltration, or further attacks.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/notebook/src/actions.tsx
+++ b/packages/notebook/src/actions.tsx
@@ -1510,12 +1510,18 @@ export namespace NotebookActions {
       return;
     }
 
-    let values = stored as nbformat.IBaseCell[];
+    const storedCells = stored as nbformat.IBaseCell[];
+    const isLocal = clipboard.matchesLastWrite(JUPYTER_CELL_MIME, stored);
+    let values = isLocal
+      ? (JSONExt.deepCopy(storedCells) as nbformat.IBaseCell[])
+      : Private.untrustSystemClipboardCells(storedCells);
+    let interactionValues = storedCells;
     if (options?.stripOutputs) {
       values = Private.stripCodeCellOutputs(values);
+      interactionValues = Private.stripCodeCellOutputs(storedCells);
     }
 
-    addCells(notebook, mode, values, true);
+    addCells(notebook, mode, values, true, interactionValues);
     void focusActiveCell(notebook);
   }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a mechanism to distinguish between internal JupyterLab clipboard operations and external system clipboard pastes. When content is pasted from the system clipboard, it is now explicitly 'untrusted', meaning any outputs in code cells will have their 'trusted' metadata set to false, preventing automatic execution of potentially malicious content. A new `matchesLastWrite` function was added to the clipboard utility to determine if the pasted data originated from the application itself.

## GHSA-p58f-9548-mpm2 · virtualenv

2026-10-01 · Python · **HIGH** 7.8 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The virtualenv activation scripts for bash and fish shells were not properly sanitizing environment variables or path components before using them. Specifically, the fish script was setting TCL_LIBRARY and TK_LIBRARY without quoting, allowing embedded commands to be executed. The bash script was echoing a path without quoting, which could lead to command execution if the path contained special characters.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious virtual environment path or manipulate environment variables to execute arbitrary commands on a user's system when they activate the virtual environment.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/virtualenv/activation/fish/activate.fish
+++ b/src/virtualenv/activation/fish/activate.fish
-  set -gx TCL_LIBRARY '__TCL_LIBRARY__'
+  set -gx TCL_LIBRARY __TCL_LIBRARY__
--- a/src/virtualenv/activation/bash/activate.sh
+++ b/src/virtualenv/activation/bash/activate.sh
-    echo "Virtual environment directory __VIRTUAL_ENV__ does not exist!" >&2
+    echo "Virtual environment directory" __VIRTUAL_ENV__ "does not exist!" >&2
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the fish activation script to remove quotes around variable assignments for TCL_LIBRARY and TK_LIBRARY, ensuring they are treated as literal strings. In the bash script, it changes the `echo` command to properly separate the string literals and the variable, preventing shell expansion of the variable's content.

## GHSA-7hhh-6rmp-j9qf · com.fasterxml.jackson.core:jackson-core

2026-10-01 · Java · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The Jackson parser's error reporting functions, specifically `_reportInvalidToken()` and `_finishErrorToken()`, did not properly enforce the `maxErrorTokenLength` limit when encountering invalid tokens. This allowed an attacker to craft a malformed JSON input that would cause the internal `StringBuilder` or `TextBuffer` to grow indefinitely as it attempted to capture the entire invalid token for error reporting, leading to excessive memory consumption.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a specially crafted, malformed JSON input to a service using Jackson, causing it to consume an unbounded amount of memory and eventually crash or become unresponsive, leading to a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/main/java/com/fasterxml/jackson/core/json/ReaderBasedJsonParser.java
+++ b/src/main/java/com/fasterxml/jackson/core/json/ReaderBasedJsonParser.java
@@ -3025,7 +3026,7 @@ protected void _reportInvalidToken(String matchedPart, String msg) throws IOExce
             } 
             ++_inputPtr;
             sb.append(c);
-            if (sb.length() >= _ioContext.errorReportConfiguration().getMaxErrorTokenLength()) {
+            if (sb.length() >= maxTokenLength) {
                 sb.append("...");
                 break;
             }
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a local variable `maxTokenLength` to explicitly retrieve the configured maximum error token length at the beginning of the error reporting methods. It then consistently checks `sb.length()` or `_textBuffer.size()` against this limit, appending '...' and breaking the loop if the limit is reached, preventing unbounded growth.

## GHSA-p6pp-m3f8-5c89 · tools.jackson.core:jackson-core

2026-10-01 · Java · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→REGEX`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the use of overly broad regular expressions (PATTERN_FLOAT and PATTERN_FLOAT_TRAILING_DOT) in the `looksLikeValidNumber` method. These regexes, particularly with quantifiers like `*` and `+` applied to character classes that could match an empty string or multiple characters, allowed for catastrophic backtracking when processing specific malformed input strings, leading to a Denial of Service.

### Impact

An attacker could supply specially crafted input strings to methods that use `looksLikeValidNumber`, causing the application to spend an excessive amount of time processing the input. This could lead to a Denial of Service (DoS) by consuming all available CPU resources and making the application unresponsive.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/main/java/tools/jackson/core/io/NumberInput.java
+++ b/src/main/java/tools/jackson/core/io/NumberInput.java
-    private final static Pattern PATTERN_FLOAT = Pattern.compile(
-          "[+-]?[0-9]*[\\.]?[0-9]+([eE][+-]?[0-9]+)?");
-
-    private final static Pattern PATTERN_FLOAT_TRAILING_DOT = Pattern.compile(
-            "[+-]?[0-9]+[\\.]");
+    public static boolean looksLikeValidNumber(final String s) {
+        if (s == null) {
+            return false;
+        }
+        final int len = s.length();
+        if (len == 0) {
+            return false;
+        }
+        int i = 0;
+        char c = s.charAt(i);
+
+        // Optional sign
+        if (c == '+' || c == '-') {
+            if (++i == len) { // sign alone
+                return false;
+            }
+            c = s.charAt(i);
+        }
+
+        // Integer part, if any
+        final int intStart = i;
+        while (c >= '0' && c <= '9') {
+            if (++i == len) { // "[+-]?[0-9]+"
+                return true;
+            }
+            c = s.charAt(i);
+        }
+        final int intDigits = i - intStart;
+
+        if (c == '.') {
+            if (++i == len) { // trailing dot only allowed after digit(s)
+                return intDigits > 0;
+            }
+            c = s.charAt(i);
+            final int fractStart = i;
+            while (c >= '0' && c <= '9') {
+                if (++i == len) {
+                    return true;
+                }
+                c = s.charAt(i);
+            }
+            if (i == fractStart) { // "1.x": no fraction digits, and not trailing dot
+                return false;
+            }
+        } else if (intDigits == 0) { // no mantissa digits at all
+            return false;
+        }
+
+        // Only an exponent may follow
+        if (c != 'e' && c != 'E') {
+            return false;
+        }
+        if (++i == len) {
+            return false;
+        }
+        c = s.charAt(i);
+        if (c == '+' || c == '-') {
+            if (++i == len) {
+                return false;
+            }
+            c = s.charAt(i);
+        }
+        while (c >= '0' && c <= '9') {
+            if (++i == len) {
+                return true;
+            }
+            c = s.charAt(i);
+        }
+        // Either no exponent digits, or trailing garbage
+        return false;
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the problematic regular expressions (`PATTERN_FLOAT` and `PATTERN_FLOAT_TRAILING_DOT`) and replaces them with a hand-rolled, iterative parsing logic within the `looksLikeValidNumber` method. This new logic explicitly checks characters and their order, avoiding the backtracking issues inherent in the previous regex-based approach.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 24 occurrences**
> Languages: Go, JavaScript, Python, PHP, Java

> → GHSA-cjw9-ghj4-fwxf

## GHSA-67c8-pqhq-4rmx · piscina

2026-10-01 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `piscina` library did not properly sanitize user-provided options when creating a `ThreadPool` or `Piscina` instance. Specifically, it used object spread syntax (`{ ...kDefaultOptions, ...options }`) which allows an attacker to inject `__proto__` properties into the options object, leading to prototype pollution.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve Remote Code Execution (RCE) by polluting the prototype of objects used internally by `piscina`. This could allow them to manipulate critical properties like `execArgv`, `loadBalancer`, or `env` which are used to spawn worker threads, ultimately leading to arbitrary code execution.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/index.ts
+++ b/src/index.ts
@@ -178,7 +179,12 @@ class ThreadPool {
 
     const filename =
       options.filename ? maybeFileURLToPath(options.filename) : null;
-    this.options = { ...kDefaultOptions, ...options, filename, maxQueue: 0 };
+    this.options = withNullPrototype({
+      ...kDefaultOptions,
+      ...options,
+      filename,
+      maxQueue: 0,
+    });
 
     if (this.options.recordTiming) {
       this.runTime = createHistogram();
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new utility function `withNullPrototype` which creates an object with a null prototype and copies properties onto it. This function is now used consistently when initializing options for `ThreadPool` and `Piscina` instances, preventing prototype pollution by ensuring that user-controlled options cannot modify the prototype chain.

## GHSA-mcm9-63f2-9j32 · devalue

2026-10-01 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `devalue` library's `uneval` function, when processing objects with many repeated long primitive strings or BigInts, would repeatedly stringify these values. This led to a quadratic time complexity in certain scenarios, as the cost of stringifying and escaping these values was incurred for each occurrence without memoization.

### Impact

An attacker could provide a specially crafted input to the `uneval` function, causing it to consume excessive CPU resources and time. This could lead to a denial of service, making the application unresponsive or crashing it.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/uneval.js
+++ b/src/uneval.js
@@ -12,6 +12,10 @@ import {
 } from './utils.js';
 
 const chars = 'abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyzABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ_$';
+// Short strings have bounded escaping/output costs per reference, so they cannot
+// cause quadratic expansion. Keep them on the original fast path rather than
+// paying for deduplication on common values such as names, IDs and status fields.
+const MIN_STRING_LENGTH = 128;
 const unsafe_chars = /[<\b\f\n\r\t\0\u2028\u2029]/g;
 const reserved =
 	/^(?:do|if|in|for|int|let|new|try|var|byte|case|char|else|enum|goto|long|this|void|with|await|break|catch|class|const|final|float|short|super|throw|while|yield|delete|double|export|import|native|return|switch|throws|typeof|boolean|default|extends|finally|package|private|abstract|continue|debugger|function|volatile|interface|protected|transient|implements|instanceof|synchronized)$/;
@@ -29,6 +33,28 @@ export function uneval(value, replacer) {
 
 	const custom = new Map();
 
+	// Only allocate a literal cache when we need to reuse an expensive primitive,
+	// including long Map keys rendered in error paths during the walk.
+	/** @type {Map<string | bigint, string> | undefined} */
+	let primitives;
+
+	/** @param {any} thing */
+	function stringify_cached_primitive(thing) {
+		if (
+			(typeof thing === 'string' && thing.length >= MIN_STRING_LENGTH) ||
+			typeof thing === 'bigint'
+		) {
+			primitives ??= new Map();
+			let literal = primitives.get(thing);
+			if (literal === undefined) {
+				literal = stringify_primitive(thing);
+				primitives.set(thing, literal);
+			}
+			return literal;
+		}
+		return stringify_primitive(thing);
+	}
+
 	/** @param {any} thing */
 	function walk(thing) {
 		if (!is_primitive(thing)) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `MIN_STRING_LENGTH` constant and a `stringify_cached_primitive` function. It now caches the stringified representation of long strings (>= 128 characters) and BigInts, preventing repeated expensive stringification. Additionally, it refines the logic for hoisting repeated primitives to ensure that only genuinely beneficial repetitions are hoisted, further optimizing performance.

## GHSA-v247-6f48-mgcj · pypdf

2026-10-01 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The previous implementation of `attachments` and related methods involved iterating through all embedded files multiple times and performing string comparisons for each access. Specifically, `_list_attachments` would iterate through all attachments to get names, and then `_get_attachments` would iterate again, potentially leading to redundant processing, especially when dealing with a large number of embedded files or files with multiple names.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a PDF document with a large number of embedded files or files with many alternative names. When a user or application processes this document, the redundant and inefficient attachment handling could lead to excessive CPU usage and long runtimes, effectively causing a denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pypdf/_doc_common.py
+++ b/pypdf/_doc_common.py
@@ -1559,81 +1559,28 @@ def xfa(self) -> Optional[dict[str, Any]]:
     @property
     def attachments(self) -> Mapping[str, list[bytes]]:
         """Mapping of attachment filenames to their content."""
+        entries: dict[str, list[EmbeddedFile]] = {}
+
+        for entry in self.attachment_list:
+            for name in entry._names:
+                entries.setdefault(name, []).append(entry)
+
         return LazyDict(
             {
-                name: (self._get_attachment_list, name)
-                for name in self._list_attachments()
+                name: (self._get_attachment_contents, attachment_entries)
+                for name, attachment_entries in entries.items()
             }
         )
 
+    @classmethod
+    def _get_attachment_contents(cls, entries: list[EmbeddedFile]) -> list[bytes]:
+        return [entry.content for entry in entries]
+
     @property
-    def attachment_list(self) -> Generator[EmbeddedFile, None, None]:
+    def attachment_list(self) -> Iterator[EmbeddedFile]:
         """Iterable of attachment objects."""
         yield from EmbeddedFile._load(self.root_object, strict=self.strict)
-
-    def _list_attachments(self) -> list[str]:
-        """
-        Retrieves the list of filenames of file attachments.
-
-        Returns:
-            list of filenames
-
-        """
-        names = []
-        for entry in self.attachment_list:
-            names.append(entry.name)
-            if (name := entry.alternative_name) != entry.name and name:
-                names.append(name)
-        return names
-
-    def _get_attachment_list(self, name: str) -> list[bytes]:
-        out = self._get_attachments(name)[name]
-        if isinstance(out, list):
-            return out
-        return [out]
-
-    def _get_attachments(
-        self, filename: Optional[str] = None
-    ) -> dict[str, Union[bytes, list[bytes]]]:
-        """
-        Retrieves all or selected file attachments of the PDF as a dictionary of file names
-        and the file data as a bytestring.
-
-        Args:
-            filename: If filename is None, then a dictionary of all attachments
-                will be returned, where the key is the filename and the value
-                is the content. Otherwise, a dictionary with just a single key
-                - the filename - and its content will be returned.
-
-        Returns:
-            dictionary of filename -> Union[bytestring or List[ByteString]]
-            If the filename exists multiple times a list of the different versions will be provided.
-
-        """
-        attachments: dict[str, Union[bytes, list[bytes]]] = {}
-        for entry in self.attachment_list:
-            names = set()
-            alternative_name = entry.alternative_name
-            if filename is not None:
-                if filename in {entry.name, alternative_name}:
-                    name = entry.name if filename == entry.name else alternative_name
-                    names.add(name)
-                else:
-                    continue
-            else:
-                names = {entry.name, alternative_name}
-
-            for name in names:
-                if name is None:
-                    continue
-                if name in attachments:
-                    if not isinstance(attachments[name], list):
-                        attachments[name] = [attachments[name]]  # type:ignore
-                    attachments[name].append(entry.content)  # type:ignore
-                else:
-                    attachments[name] = entry.content
-        return attachments
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors the attachment handling logic. It now pre-processes the list of `EmbeddedFile` objects once to create a mapping of names to a list of `EmbeddedFile` instances. This mapping is then used by a `LazyDict` to retrieve content on demand, avoiding repeated iterations and string comparisons, thus optimizing performance.

## GHSA-x5rw-q4pp-hg5g · devalue

2026-10-01 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `stringifyAsync` function in `devalue` could encounter an error during object traversal before it reached an awaited promise. If this happened, the promise returned by `stringifyAsync` would remain pending, and the error would be thrown as an unhandled rejection, even if the caller had a `.catch()` handler on the returned promise.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially cause an application using `devalue` to crash due to an unhandled promise rejection, leading to a denial of service. This could disrupt the application's normal operation.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/stringify.js
+++ b/src/stringify.js
@@ -152,6 +152,11 @@ function run(async, value, reducers, options) {
 				const i = flatten(value, index);
 				if (i < 0) stringified[index] = i;
 			});
+
+			// Promises are awaited sequentially, and traversal may throw before
+			// reaching them. Handle rejections immediately, but keep the original
+			// promise so awaiting it still propagates the error to the caller.
+			str.catch(() => {});
 		} else {
 			const tag = ops.tagOf(thing);
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds a `.catch(() => {})` handler to the internal promise (`str`) that is used for sequential awaiting. This ensures that any rejections occurring during traversal are immediately handled internally, preventing them from becoming unhandled rejections, while still allowing the original error to propagate to the caller's `.catch()` on the main returned promise.

## GHSA-x78j-v8h9-3j2q · virtualenv

2026-10-01 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The `activate.bat` script in virtualenv did not properly sanitize user-controlled input, specifically the `--prompt` argument and certain path-related environment variables. Batch scripts interpret special characters like `&`, `|`, `<`, `>`, `^`, `!`, and `"` as command operators or delimiters, even within quoted strings for variable assignments. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary commands by crafting a malicious string that would break out of the intended variable assignment context.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary commands on a Windows system where a virtual environment with a specially crafted prompt or path was activated. This could lead to full system compromise, data exfiltration, or denial of service.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/virtualenv/activation/batch/__init__.py
+++ b/src/virtualenv/activation/batch/__init__.py
@@ -53,4 +53,13 @@
 
     @staticmethod
     def quote(string: str) -> str:
-        return string
+        string = string.replace("%", "%%")
+        for operator in _CMD_OPERATORS:
+            string = string.replace(operator, " ")
+        return collapse_line_boundaries(string)
 
     def instantiate_template(self, replacements: dict[str, str], template: str, creator: Creator) -> str:
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `quote` method in `BatchActivator` to sanitize strings by replacing command operators and line boundaries with spaces, and doubling percent signs to prevent variable expansion. It also adds a check to prevent the creation of batch activation scripts if critical path variables contain characters that cannot be safely quoted in batch scripts, logging a warning instead.
